# Voices of the Church PR V1: the Catena Aurea on the Readings Step

> **For agentic workers:** REQUIRED SUB-SKILL: Use superpowers:subagent-driven-development (recommended) or superpowers:executing-plans to implement this plan task-by-task. Steps use checkbox (`- [ ]`) syntax for tracking.

**Goal:** Ship Voices of the Church V1 (owner's answers of 2026-10-01, planning answers and source decision of 2026-10-05; planning answer 8: "V1, the rights check, the text import and the Readings panel"). After it merges, step 1 of the Service Builder (`/builder/readings`) shows a **Voices of the Church** panel under the Gospel reading's row: closed, its button reads "Voices of the Church" and "19 quotations on these verses"; open, it shows each section of Thomas Aquinas's Catena Aurea that shares a verse with the passage, with its verses and a link to its printed pages in the scan, then each father's comment exactly as the 1841-45 Oxford printing has it (the father's name, the margin reference such as "Chrys. Hom. lxx.", the text with its italics), and the credit to the edition. A section nobody has checked against the page images yet says "Not yet transcribed for this passage." The passage is a bulletin reading from a Gospel; when neither bulletin reading is one, the Sunday's lectionary Gospel, labeled "From the Gospel for this Sunday: Matthew 22:15-22" (planning answer 1). The text is four data files the app ships with (`backend/data/catena/`): every section of the four Gospels (Matthew 277, Mark 104, Luke 245, John 190) with its verses, volume and pages, and the text of the five sections checked while planning: Matthew 22:15-22 (the owner's test Sunday, October 18, 2026), 22:34-40 and 22:41-46 (October 25), 23:1-4 and 23:5-12 (November 1), 92 quotations. No database change, no AI, no new package or variable; production Streamlit (branch `streamlit-frozen`) is untouched. V2 ("Bring this into the liturgy" and the "after {Name}" credit) is designed in the spec and not built here.

**Architecture:** `backend/catena.py` (new, pure) reads a Gospel's file once (`lru_cache`), checks it against the format (`CatenaDataError`) and answers `sections_for(reference)` with the app's own reference parser (`scripture_refs.parse_refs`, the hymn matcher's) and `credit(gospel, sections)`. `backend/data/catena/{matthew,mark,luke,john}.json` hold one section a line (a checked section's comments one a line), text only for checked sections; `backend/data/catena/README.md` is the checking checklist. `backend/scripts/catena_import.py` (new, standard library, never run by the app) turns archive.org's ABBYY OCR of the eight volumes into drafts (`fetch`, `draft`, `show`) and rewrites a data file keeping its checked sections (`index --checked FILE`). The API: `usecases/voices.py` and `api/routes/voices.py`, `GET /voices?reference=` (user-scoped like `GET /translations`; `Cache-Control: private, max-age=3600`; a 422 naming `reference` for a passage not in a Gospel); `VoicesOut`, `VoiceSectionOut`, `VoiceCommentOut` in `api/schemas.py`; OpenAPI regenerated. Frontend: `lib/voices.ts` (`voicesPassage`: which passage, from the draft's effective bulletin readings and the lectionary answer step 1 already holds), `lib/queries/voices.ts` (`useVoices`, key `["ref", "voices", reference]`, an hour), `components/builder/readings/voices-panel.tsx` (the panel, on the kit's `Collapsible`), placed by `readings-list.tsx` under the Gospel's row (`ReadingRow` gains `children`) or after the list.

**Tech Stack:** Python 3.11 (`.venv`), FastAPI 0.141, Pydantic 2, pytest; Next 16, React 19, TypeScript 5, Base UI, TanStack Query 5, Vitest 3 with Testing Library; openapi-typescript for `schema.d.ts`.

**Source documents:**
- Spec ("S"): `docs/superpowers/specs/2026-10-05-voices-of-the-church-design.md` (V1 and V2).
- Owner decisions ("D"): `docs/superpowers/specs/2026-10-01-voices-of-the-church-decisions.md`: the answers of 2026-10-01 (1-8), "Planning answers (2026-10-05)" (1-9) and "Source decision (2026-10-05)" (1-3), all binding.
- Rights check: `docs/superpowers/specs/2026-10-05-voices-rights-check.md` (YELLOW; the source decision chose the archive.org scans of the 1841-45 printing).
- Foundations ("F"): `docs/superpowers/specs/2026-09-25-migration-foundations-design.md` §1.2 (user-scoped routes), §1.5 (errors), §1.8 (rate limits, plain `def`), §2.2 (layers), §4.4 (keys), §4.5 (`apiFetch`), §4.8 (44 px, no raw HTML).
- Format models: `docs/superpowers/plans/2026-10-03-printed-bulletin-3.md` and `2026-10-02-printed-bulletin-2a.md`.
- Facts checked for this plan (branch `claude/slice-2-plan-4q33le` at `12a0c58` = `origin/main` `875aefc` (PR #50) plus "Runbook: printed bulletin PR 3b record", the planning answers, the rights check and the source decision; then this plan's commits; 2026-10-05): backend `1487 passed, 23 skipped`; frontend `734 passed` in 88 files; typecheck and lint clean. Step 1 gets the lectionary through `useLectionaryLookup()` (`GET /lectionary/readings`; `usecases/lectionary.py` merges the Vanderbilt CSV, `vanderbilt_lectionary.py`, with Lectio); its answer for 2026-10-18 is one set, "Twenty-First Sunday after Pentecost": Isaiah 45:1-7, Psalm 96:1-9 (10-13), 1 Thessalonians 1:1-10, Matthew 22:15-22, so the automatic bulletin readings are Isaiah and 1 Thessalonians and the panel falls back to the lectionary's Gospel. The next Sundays (`usecases.lectionary.readings_for_date`, run 2026-10-05): October 25 Matthew 22:34-46; November 1 Matthew 23:1-12 (the default set, Twenty-Third Sunday after Pentecost; the other set, All Saints Day, Matthew 5:1-12); November 8 Matthew 25:1-13; November 15 Matthew 25:14-30; November 22 (Reign of Christ) Matthew 25:31-46; November 29 (Advent 1) Mark 13:24-37. `scripture_refs.parse_refs` reads "Luke 15:1-3, 11b-32" as two spans and "Matthew 22" as the whole chapter. A frontend request without a handler fails its test (`test/fake-api.ts`), so the readings step's and the builder shell's tests gain a `GET /voices` handler. `.gitignore`'s `data/*.json` matches only the root `data/`: `git check-ignore backend/data/catena/matthew.json` exits 1.

## Global Constraints

"T<n>" means Task n of this plan.

### Commands and process
- Run commands from the repo root; the working directory resets between commands. Frontend as `(cd frontend && …)`. No foreground `sleep`.
- Backend: one or more files `.venv/bin/python -m pytest -q <paths> 2>&1 | tail -3` (or `| tail -1` where a step says so); the suite `.venv/bin/python -m pytest -q | tail -1`. Frontend: one or more files `(cd frontend && npx vitest run <paths> 2>&1 | grep -E "^ +× |\[ src/|Tests ")` (a file that cannot load shows as `FAIL … [ src/… ]`; the `×` lines may come in another order than quoted); the suite `(cd frontend && npx vitest run 2>&1 | grep -E "^ +× |FAIL|Test Files|Tests ")`; then `(cd frontend && npx tsc --noEmit >/dev/null 2>&1; echo "typecheck $?"; npm run lint >/dev/null 2>&1; echo "lint $?")`.
- The API changes (T3), so T3 regenerates the snapshot and the types in the same commit: `.venv/bin/python backend/scripts/export_openapi.py` then `(cd frontend && npm run gen:api)`. Never edit `openapi.json` or `schema.d.ts` by hand. T3 also adds the `Voices` type names, so typecheck stays clean at every commit.
- No new package, no new variable, no migration, no workflow change.
- Branch `claude/slice-2-plan-4q33le`, at `12a0c58` plus this plan's commits (`WIP spec and plan: …`, `WIP plan: …`, `Spec and plan: Voices of the Church V1` and any later plan commit), then T1-T6. Stage files by name (paths with parentheses in single quotes); `.claude/` stays untracked.
- `main` is protected (`backend`, `backend-postgres`, `frontend`, up to date). Merge only with `gh pr merge <N> --merge -R bbrown62450/church`, only on the owner's explicit yes.
- Every commit message has a subject, a body and, as its last paragraph (a separate `-m`), these two lines:
  `Co-Authored-By: Claude Opus 5.5 <noreply@anthropic.com>`
  `Claude-Session: https://claude.ai/code/session_01LhHxTA5m6dKphy5MuKjHCS`
- TDD: write the failing test first and see it fail as quoted.
- **Backup push after every task** (standing rule): the controller runs `git push origin claude/slice-2-plan-4q33le` after each task's commit (never `--force`, never a rebase; if the push is rejected, `git pull --no-rebase origin claude/slice-2-plan-4q33le` and push again; on a network error retry after 2, 4, 8 and 16 s). A fix asked for by a review is a new commit, `Fix: <what> (Task <n> review)`. The container can restart and lose uncommitted work: commit as soon as a task's checks pass.
- The PR body ends with `🤖 Generated with [Claude Code](https://claude.com/claude-code)` and then `https://claude.ai/code/session_01LhHxTA5m6dKphy5MuKjHCS`, and includes its tests line: `Tests: backend 1487 → 1561 passed, 23 skipped (unchanged); frontend 734 → 751 in 88 → 91 files`.
- New prose for the owner has no em dashes and no flattery. New user-facing copy is exactly the list in clarification 10 and has no em dashes; the Catena's own text keeps the printing's em dashes (clarification 3).
- No church id, email address, token, database URL, real person's name, address, phone or email in any doc, commit, test or record. The `checked.by` of a data file names the checker's role ("Claude (Voices V1 planning session)", "the owner"), never an email address.
- Ask the owner before any push to a PR, PR creation, marking ready, merging, or any production or settings action. Owner steps go one at a time, in plain words.

### How the file directives below read
As in the printed bulletin plans: **Create `path`:** the block is the whole file; **Append to `path`:** the block is added at the end of the file (a leading empty line in the block is part of it); **In `path`, replace:** the first block occurs exactly once in the file and is replaced by the block after **with:**. Blocks are fenced with four backticks and applied in the order written (top to bottom within a file, so an anchor is unique in the file as the previous directives left it). A directive that does not match exactly once is a stop: find why before changing anything. No file is deleted. A block's final newline is the file's.

### Layering and code rules (carried)
- `catena` and `usecases.voices` import no FastAPI, Starlette or Streamlit (`test_no_streamlit_in_core.py` gains both, T3); the route is a plain `def` with one usecase call, no try/except (F §2.2 rule 1). The route is user-scoped: `get_current_user`, never `require_church` (`test_route_guards.USER_SCOPED` gains it).
- `backend/scripts/catena_import.py` is a developer's tool: standard library and `catena` only; the app never imports it; its network fetch (`fetch`) runs only by hand.
- No log line carries the Catena's text or the reference (the request log is unchanged).
- Pages and components never call `apiFetch`: the panel uses `useVoices` through `useApi()` (F §4.5). Touch targets 44 px below `md`; text wraps at 375 px; no raw HTML (the italics are React `<em>` elements built from `*...*`; F §4.8).

1. **Standing permission** for safety and reliability fixes with no owner-visible change (carried). Each use is a numbered clarification.
2. **Production Streamlit** (branch `streamlit-frozen`) is frozen and retired for real use; merges never reach it.
3. **`main` is branch-protected**; Railway and Vercel deploy on merge.
4. **D, all sections, binding.** From the answers of 2026-10-01: the fathers verse by verse on the day's Gospel from the Catena Aurea in the 1841-45 Oxford translation, on the Readings step (1(1)); for preparation only, never printed (3); only the verses read get quotations (6). From the planning answers of 2026-10-05: the lectionary Gospel when no chosen reading is a Gospel, labeled (1); a data file the app ships with, no database change, the source credited on the panel (2); a collapsible panel under the Gospel reading, collapsed with a count (3); the 1841 English exactly as printed (4); two PRs, V1 here (8); the test Sunday October 18, 2026, Matthew 22:15-22 (9). From the source decision: the archive.org scans of the 1841-45 printing for all four Gospels, cleaned automatically, each section checked against the page images before the app shows it, "Not yet transcribed for this passage." otherwise (1); grouped by the Catena's sections, a section shown when it overlaps the verses read (2).
5. **V2** (planning answers 5 and 6, "Bring this into the liturgy" and the "after {Name}" credit) is designed in S and gets its own plan after V1 is live.

## Spec clarifications

The owner's answers win over S and F; the code wins over both where they disagree. **[owner-visible]** items are put to the owner in "Questions for the owner" (each written as recommended).

1. **[owner-visible] What V1 ships** (planning answer 8; source decision 1). The four data files, `GET /voices`, the panel on step 1, the import tool and its checklist, and five checked sections (Matthew 22:15-22, 22:34-40, 22:41-46, 23:1-4, 23:5-12; 92 quotations; "Checks of the text made while planning" below). Not here: V2, footnotes, the Gospel text the Catena prints before each section (the step already shows the reading), any change to the bulletin or the Word files (answer 3).
2. **[owner-visible] Text only for checked sections** (source decision 1; question 1). The files list every section of the four Gospels (Matthew 277, Mark 104, Luke 245, John 190) with its verses, volume, printed pages and scan pages, so the panel can say which section is not yet transcribed and link to its pages; only a checked section has text. Uncorrected OCR never enters the repository or the app. The other choice, shipping the whole cleaned OCR (about 4 MB) hidden until checked, puts known-wrong text one bug away from the screen and makes every check's diff large.
3. **[owner-visible] "Exactly as printed"** (planning answer 4; question 3). Kept: every word and its spelling ("shew", "pourtrayed", "offence", "Saviour"), the capitals, the punctuation (the printing's em dashes inside a comment included: "thoughts—with all thy soul"), "æ" ("Cæsar", "Judæa", "Quæst."), the italics (the Gospel's words quoted in a comment), the label as printed ("Pseudo-Chrys.", "Jerome;"), the margin references and notes, a printed paragraph inside one father's comment, the marks of the printing's marginal variants ("good¹" with "¹ alia re frui." in the margin). Not kept (typesetting): line and page breaks; a word the line end broke is joined ("popu-lace"), a word hyphenated anyway keeps its hyphen ("first-fruits", "self-satisfied"); the space before ";", ":", "?", "!" ("Thou ?" is "Thou?"); curly quotes typed straight; tight margin spacing ("Ps.11,5." is "Ps. 11, 5."). Not shown in V1: the editors' footnotes and their marks (Matthew 23:9's note "a", the editors' remark on the soul's creation, is not a father's word), page signatures ("VOL. I. 3 D"). A connective paragraph Aquinas prints between two comments ("It follows, *On these two commandments hang all the Law and the Prophets.*", after Hilary on 22:39) stays where printed, as the second paragraph of the comment before it.
4. **[owner-visible] The name shown** (planning answer 3, "each with the father's name and work"; question 4). Each quotation is headed by the father's full name (`father`: "Chrysostom", "Pseudo-Chrysostom", "Augustine", "The Gloss", "Rabanus Maurus", "Gregory the Great", …; `catena.FATHERS`), then the margin reference beside it as printed ("Chrys. Hom. lxx.", "Aug. de Doctr. Christ. i. 22.", "Gloss. ord."; none when the printing has none). The printed label ("Chrys.", "Jerome;", "Id.") is kept in the data for V2 and for checking; "Id." ("the same") shows the father before it.
5. **[owner-visible] Grouped by section** (source decision 2). A section shows when it shares at least one verse with the passage; the button counts the checked quotations of all of them. Sections show in verse order (the Catena prints the Beatitudes in the Vulgate's order, 5:5 before 5:4; the panel shows 5:4 first). Two sections may share a verse (the Catena splits some verses): both show.
6. **[owner-visible] Which passage** (planning answer 1). The bulletin's two readings as step 1 resolves them (picked or automatic; `effectivePicks`), New Testament first, then Old Testament: the first whose first alternative is in Matthew, Mark, Luke or John gives the passage, that alternative ("Mark 1:1-8 or Luke 3:1-6" asks for Mark 1:1-8). Else the Sunday's lectionary Gospel: the lectionary answer step 1 holds for the draft's date, its set the draft uses (`selectedSetIndex`) or the default set, its first Gospel line, labeled "From the Gospel for this Sunday: {reference}". Else no panel (no lectionary answer yet, another date's, none for the date, or a set with no Gospel). On October 18, 2026 the automatic readings are Isaiah and 1 Thessalonians, so the label shows; picking Matthew 22:15-22 as the New Testament reading removes it.
7. **[owner-visible] The panel** (planning answer 3; S "The panel"; question 5). Under the row of the passage's line in **Readings** (after the list when that line is not one of the rows, for example typed lines without the lectionary's Gospel). Closed at first; it stays as the member leaves it while step 1 is open, and is closed again when the step is opened anew. The button (44 px or taller, full width, `aria-expanded` from the kit's `Collapsible`) says "Voices of the Church" and the count. Open: each section's verses as a heading, "Printed pages 748-752, Vol. I, St. Matthew, Part III (1842)" as a link to the scan's viewer in a new tab (44 px tall on phones; "(opens in a new tab)" for screen readers), then its quotations as a list, or "Not yet transcribed for this passage."; the credit last. The quotations are 16 px with relaxed line height; italics as `<em>`; a margin note as "In the margin: 1 Tim. 4, 3." The panel asks the server when the step shows a passage (the count needs it) and keeps the answer an hour.
8. **[owner-visible] The credit** (planning answer 2; question 2). Built by the server: "From the Catena Aurea of Thomas Aquinas, vol. I, St. Matthew, translated by Mark Pattison, edited by John Henry Newman (Oxford: John Henry Parker, 1841-42). Scanned from the University of Toronto's copy at archive.org." Mark: "vol. II, St. Mark, translated by John Dobree Dalgairns … 1842"; Luke: "vol. III, St. Luke, translated by Thomas Dudley Ryder … 1843"; John: "vol. IV, St. John, translator not named in the volume … 1845". The copies are the shown sections' (Matthew 11-21: "Saint Mary's College of California's copy"). Never CCEL's "William Whiston" (rights check §2).
9. **The API** (S "The API"). `GET /voices?reference=` (1-200 characters): user-scoped (`get_current_user`; `X-Church-Id` ignored), like `GET /translations`; no rate-limit bucket (no AI, no database, no upstream; the file is read once); `Cache-Control: private, max-age=3600`. `200 VoicesOut {reference, gospel, quotation_count, credit, sections: [{id, reference, pages ("748-752"), volume ("Vol. I, St. Matthew, Part III (1842)"), scan_url, status ("checked" | "unchecked"), comments: [{label, father, work, text, notes}]}]}`. A reference with no Gospel passage: 422 `invalid_request` naming `reference`, "Choose a passage from Matthew, Mark, Luke or John."; missing or too long: the usual 422 ("Required.", "Too long (max 200 characters)."). The passages of the first Gospel named count.
10. **[owner-visible] Every new user-facing string** (no em dashes). The panel: "Voices of the Church"; "{n} quotations on these verses", "1 quotation on these verses"; "Not yet transcribed for this passage."; "From the Gospel for this Sunday: {reference}"; "Printed pages {pages}, {volume}" (for screen readers with " (opens in a new tab)"); "In the margin: {notes}"; "Loading the fathers' comments…"; "The fathers' comments couldn't be loaded." with "Try again" (for screen readers "Try again: Voices of the Church"); the credit (clarification 8). From the server: "Choose a passage from Matthew, Mark, Luke or John." The fathers' names (`catena.FATHERS`, first letter capitalized as a heading: "The Gloss", "A Greek expositor").
11. **The data format** (S "The data"). One file per Gospel, `{"format": 1, "gospel", "sections": [...]}`, one section a line and a checked section's comments one a line (so a check's diff shows what changed); a section `{id, start, end, volume, pages, leaves, status}` and, when checked, `checked {on, by}` and `comments`; a comment `{label, father, work, text, notes}`, `*...*` italic, `\n\n` a printed paragraph, nothing else. `catena.parse` refuses a file that breaks it (the format and Gospel; the id from the verses, `-2` for a second section on the same verses, unique; sections in verse order; the volume's Gospel; a checked section's date, checker and comments; an unchecked section's lack of text; a known father; text without spaces at its ends, single line breaks, double spaces or an unpaired `*`). `test_catena_data.py` loads the four shipped files and pins their counts and the checked sections.
12. **The import tool** (S "The import tool"). Run by a developer with a cache directory outside the repo; never by the app, CI or Railway (its tests use invented pages). It reads archive.org's ABBYY OCR (`<item>_abbyy.gz`, about 100 MB for the eight volumes; the only OCR file that keeps each character's place, italics and small capitals). Splitting and cleanup as S describes; the drafts are working copies, never committed. `index` keeps every checked section of the file and adds `--checked` files, and a drafted section that shares a verse with a checked one gives way to it, so a checker can correct a section's verses.
13. **[owner-visible] Checking and the record** (source decision 1; question 6). The five sections were checked word by word against the page images while planning ("Checks of the text made while planning"); each carries `checked: {"on": "2026-10-05", "by": "Claude (Voices V1 planning session), word by word against the page images"}`. `backend/data/catena/README.md` is the checklist for later checks (the tool's commands, what to compare, the record, the test to update); the owner's phone check reads one quotation of 22:15-22 against the scan (T8). Not yet checked: All Saints Day (Matthew 5:1-12, November 1's other set), November 8 (25:1-13), 15 (25:14-30), 22 (25:31-46) and the rest of the four Gospels.
14. **Privacy and cost** (S "Privacy, cost, rate limits"). Static public-domain text: no church or personal data, no AI, no new bucket, no log line beyond the request log. Memory: the four files, about 165 KB in all, read once each.
15. **Docs** (T6, T8). `docs/manual-verification.md` gains "## Voices of the Church" at the end with items 1-7; `test_slice1_docs.py`'s pin of the last `##` headings gains it (the last nine instead of eight). The runbook record is T8's (`### Voices of the Church V1 record`, before `## Backups`, in its own records PR). The printed bulletin PR 3b record (`751b110`) rides along in this PR.
16. **What does not change.** The bulletin, the Word files and the printed bulletin (answer 3); the draft (no version change: the open state is the page's, not the draft's); the database and the migrations; the lectionary and passages routes; `scripture_refs.py`; the workflows, `package.json`, `requirements*.txt`; `app.py`, Streamlit.
17. **[owner-visible] Rolling back** (T8 Step R). Nothing else depends on V1: no migration, no draft change, no stored data. Reverting the merge commit removes the panel, the route and the files; a page still open meanwhile shows "The fathers' comments couldn't be loaded." on its panel until reloaded.

### Risks
- **The tool's split is a heuristic.** An unchecked section's verses can be off where the OCR missed a verse number or a heading (the tool warns: Matthew 16, Mark 7, Luke 23, John 23 sections, mostly a missed verse number or the Catena's own order). Only the heading of a "Not yet transcribed" section shows it; a checker corrects the verses when checking, and `index` keeps the checked section.
- **Checking takes time.** About 3-6 minutes a printed page here (a Sunday's Gospel is 3-10 pages; the five sections took 26 pages). Each Sunday after November 1 needs its sections checked before it, or the panel says "Not yet transcribed" (question 6).
- **A checker's mistake.** The check is by eye; the README's checklist, a second pass over the italics and the margin, and the owner's phone check reduce it. The data tests catch format slips and the OCR slips the tool knows, not a wrong word.
- **The margin references are the hardest part.** The OCR mixes them into the line or loses their second line; every one of the five sections needed margin corrections (the check record lists them).
- **archive.org's addresses** (the scan viewer link) could change; the link is a convenience, the text does not depend on it.
- **Matthew Part II** (chapters 11-21) is a different library's copy whose OCR has no small capitals and more slips; checking there takes longer.
- **The panel's request on step 1.** Every visit to step 1 with a Gospel asks `GET /voices` once an hour at most (cached by TanStack Query and the browser); the answer is a few KB to about 25 KB (Matthew 22:34-46: two sections, 36 quotations).

## The server and the text (T1-T3)

### Task 1: The Catena's data files and their format (D planning answers 2, 4; source decision 1, 2; S "The data"; clarifications 2, 3, 4, 11, 13)

**Files:**
- Create: `backend/catena.py`, `backend/data/catena/matthew.json`, `backend/data/catena/mark.json`, `backend/data/catena/luke.json`, `backend/data/catena/john.json`, `backend/data/catena/README.md`
- Test: `backend/tests/test_catena.py`, `backend/tests/test_catena_data.py`

The data files were made with T2's tool from archive.org's OCR (`fetch`, `draft`, `index`) and the five checked sections were added with `index --checked` after their checks ("Checks of the text made while planning"); here they are written as they are, so the build needs no download. One section a line; a checked section's comments one a line.

- [ ] **Step 1: Write the failing tests**

**Create `backend/tests/test_catena.py`:**

````python
"""backend/catena.py: the data files' format, the overlap with a reading, the credit (Voices V1;
spec "The data", "The API").

The format checks run on small invented files; the shipped files are test_catena_data.py's.
"""
import copy

import pytest

import catena
from catena import CatenaDataError

COMMENT = {"label": "Jerome;", "father": "Jerome", "work": None,
           "text": "Lately under Cæsar Augustus, *Then went the Pharisees.*", "notes": []}
CHECKED = {"id": "matthew-22-15-22", "start": [22, 15], "end": [22, 22], "volume": "mt3",
           "pages": [748, 752], "leaves": [19, 23], "status": "checked",
           "checked": {"on": "2026-10-05", "by": "a test"}, "comments": [COMMENT]}
UNCHECKED = {"id": "matthew-22-23-33", "start": [22, 23], "end": [22, 33], "volume": "mt3",
             "pages": [752, 760], "leaves": [23, 31], "status": "unchecked"}


def data(*sections):
    return {"format": 1, "gospel": "Matthew", "sections": [copy.deepcopy(s) for s in sections]}


def test_a_file_reads_as_sections_with_their_comments():
    checked, unchecked = catena.parse(data(CHECKED, UNCHECKED), "Matthew")
    assert checked.reference == "Matthew 22:15-22"
    assert checked.volume.identifier == "catenaurecommpt301thomuoft"
    assert (checked.pages, checked.leaves, checked.checked["on"]) == ((748, 752), (19, 23), "2026-10-05")
    assert checked.comments == (catena.Comment("Jerome;", "Jerome", None, COMMENT["text"], ()),)
    assert (unchecked.reference, unchecked.checked, unchecked.comments) == ("Matthew 22:23-33", None, ())


def breaks(change):
    broken = data(CHECKED, UNCHECKED)
    change(broken)
    with pytest.raises(CatenaDataError):
        catena.parse(broken, "Matthew")


@pytest.mark.parametrize("change", [
    lambda d: d.update(format=2),
    lambda d: d.update(gospel="Mark"),
    lambda d: d["sections"][0].update(id="matthew-22-15-21"),
    lambda d: d["sections"][1].update(id="matthew-22-15-22", start=[22, 15], end=[22, 22]),
    lambda d: d["sections"].reverse(),
    lambda d: d["sections"][0].update(volume="mk"),
    lambda d: d["sections"][0].update(status="draft"),
    lambda d: d["sections"][0].update(checked={"on": "5 Oct", "by": "a test"}),
    lambda d: d["sections"][0].update(comments=[]),
    lambda d: d["sections"][1].update(comments=[COMMENT]),
    lambda d: d["sections"][0]["comments"][0].update(father="Jerome of Stridon"),
    lambda d: d["sections"][0]["comments"][0].update(text=" Lately"),
    lambda d: d["sections"][0]["comments"][0].update(text="Lately\nunder"),
    lambda d: d["sections"][0]["comments"][0].update(text="Lately *under"),
    lambda d: d["sections"][0]["comments"][0].update(work=""),
    lambda d: d["sections"][0]["comments"][0].pop("notes"),
], ids=["format", "gospel", "id", "id twice", "order", "volume", "status", "date", "no comments",
        "text unchecked", "father", "spaces", "line break", "italics", "work", "keys"])
def test_a_file_that_breaks_the_format_is_refused(change):
    breaks(change)


def test_a_printed_paragraph_and_a_second_section_on_the_same_verses_are_allowed():
    paragraphs = copy.deepcopy(CHECKED)
    paragraphs["comments"][0]["text"] = "First paragraph.\n\nSecond *paragraph*."
    again = dict(copy.deepcopy(UNCHECKED), id="matthew-22-23-33-2")
    sections = catena.parse(data(paragraphs, UNCHECKED, again), "Matthew")
    assert [s.id for s in sections] == ["matthew-22-15-22", "matthew-22-23-33", "matthew-22-23-33-2"]
    assert sections[0].comments[0].text == "First paragraph.\n\nSecond *paragraph*."


def test_a_section_across_chapters_has_both_in_its_id_and_reference():
    across = dict(copy.deepcopy(UNCHECKED), id="matthew-22-46-23-2", start=[22, 46], end=[23, 2])
    (section,) = catena.parse(data(across), "Matthew")
    assert (section.id, section.reference) == ("matthew-22-46-23-2", "Matthew 22:46-23:2")
    assert catena.section_id("Matthew", (22, 46), (23, 2)) == "matthew-22-46-23-2"
    assert catena.section_id("John", (1, 14), (1, 14)) == "john-1-14-14"


@pytest.mark.parametrize("reference, ids", [
    ("Matthew 22:15-22", ["matthew-22-15-22"]),
    ("Matt 22:15-22", ["matthew-22-15-22"]),
    ("Matthew 22:20-25", ["matthew-22-15-22", "matthew-22-23-33"]),
    ("Matthew 22:1-14", []),
    ("Matthew 22", ["matthew-22-15-22", "matthew-22-23-33"]),
    ("Matthew 22:15-16, 30", ["matthew-22-15-22", "matthew-22-23-33"]),
    ("Isaiah 45:1-7", []),
    ("", []),
])
def test_the_sections_that_share_a_verse_with_the_reading(monkeypatch, reference, ids):
    monkeypatch.setattr(catena, "load", lambda gospel: catena.parse(data(CHECKED, UNCHECKED), "Matthew"))
    assert [s.id for s in catena.sections_for(reference)] == ids


def test_the_gospel_and_its_passages_come_from_the_first_gospel_named():
    assert catena.gospel_of("Psalm 96:1-9; Matthew 22:15-22") == "Matthew"
    assert catena.gospel_of("Mark 1:1-8 or Luke 3:1-6") == "Mark"
    assert catena.parse_gospel_reference("Mark 1:1-8 or Luke 3:1-6") == [((1, 1), (1, 8))]
    assert catena.gospel_of("1 John 3:1-3") is None
    assert catena.parse_gospel_reference("Acts 2:1-21") == []


def test_the_credit_names_the_edition_the_translator_and_the_copies_scanned():
    (section,) = catena.parse(data(CHECKED), "Matthew")
    assert catena.credit("Matthew", (section,)) == (
        "From the Catena Aurea of Thomas Aquinas, vol. I, St. Matthew, translated by Mark Pattison, "
        "edited by John Henry Newman (Oxford: John Henry Parker, 1841-42). Scanned from the "
        "University of Toronto's copy at archive.org.")
    assert catena.credit("John") == (
        "From the Catena Aurea of Thomas Aquinas, vol. IV, St. John, translator not named in the volume, "
        "edited by John Henry Newman (Oxford: John Henry Parker, 1845). Scanned from the University of "
        "Toronto's copy at archive.org.")
    assert "Saint Mary's College of California's" in catena.credit("Matthew")
    assert "Whiston" not in "".join(catena.credit(g) for g in catena.GOSPELS)


def test_the_scan_addresses():
    volume = catena.volume("mt3")
    assert catena.page_image_url(volume, 19) == "https://archive.org/download/catenaurecommpt301thomuoft/page/n19.jpg"
    assert catena.page_view_url(volume, 19) == "https://archive.org/details/catenaurecommpt301thomuoft/page/n19/mode/1up"
````

**Create `backend/tests/test_catena_data.py`:**

````python
"""The Catena's data files as shipped (backend/data/catena/; Voices V1 spec "The data").

Every file loads and passes the format checks, holds every section the import tool found, and
holds text only for the sections checked against the page images (the V1 plan's check record).
A newly checked section changes CHECKED here in the same commit.
"""
import json

import pytest

import catena

SECTIONS = {"Matthew": 277, "Mark": 104, "Luke": 245, "John": 190}
CHECKED = {
    "Matthew": ["matthew-22-15-22", "matthew-22-34-40", "matthew-22-41-46", "matthew-23-1-4", "matthew-23-5-12"],
    "Mark": [],
    "Luke": [],
    "John": [],
}
# The quotations of each checked section (the V1 plan's check record).
QUOTATIONS = {"matthew-22-15-22": 19, "matthew-22-34-40": 23, "matthew-22-41-46": 13, "matthew-23-1-4": 11,
              "matthew-23-5-12": 26}


@pytest.mark.parametrize("gospel", catena.GOSPELS)
def test_every_file_loads_with_every_section_and_text_only_where_checked(gospel):
    sections = catena.load(gospel)
    assert len(sections) == SECTIONS[gospel]
    assert [s.id for s in sections if s.checked] == CHECKED[gospel]
    assert {s.id: len(s.comments) for s in sections if s.checked} == {i: QUOTATIONS[i] for i in CHECKED[gospel]}
    assert not any(s.comments for s in sections if not s.checked)
    assert {s.volume.gospel for s in sections} == {gospel}


@pytest.mark.parametrize("gospel", catena.GOSPELS)
def test_the_file_is_one_section_a_line_and_one_checked_comment_a_line(gospel):
    text = catena.data_path(gospel).read_text(encoding="utf-8")
    lines = text.splitlines()
    assert lines[0] == f'{{"format": 1, "gospel": "{gospel}", "sections": ['
    assert lines[-1] == "]}" and text.endswith("]}\n")
    raw = json.loads(text)
    checked_comments = sum(len(s.get("comments", [])) for s in raw["sections"])
    assert len(lines) == 2 + len(raw["sections"]) + checked_comments + len(CHECKED[gospel])


def test_matthew_22_15_22_is_the_printed_text_of_pages_748_to_752():
    (section,) = catena.sections_for("Matthew 22:15-22")
    assert (section.pages, section.leaves, section.volume.year) == ((748, 752), (19, 23), 1842)
    assert section.checked["on"] == "2026-10-05"
    labels = [c.label for c in section.comments]
    assert labels == ["Pseudo-Chrys.", "Gloss.", "Jerome;", "Chrys.", "Pseudo-Chrys.", "Gloss.", "Chrys.",
                      "Jerome;", "Chrys.", "Pseudo-Chrys.", "Jerome;", "Pseudo-Chrys.", "Jerome;", "Hilary;",
                      "Chrys.", "Jerome;", "Hilary;", "Origen;", "Jerome;"]
    assert [c.work for c in section.comments if c.work] == ["Gloss. ord.", "Chrys. Hom. lxx.", "Gloss. non occ."]
    assert section.comments[17].notes == ("1 Tim. 4, 3.",)
    assert section.comments[0].text.startswith("As when one seeks to dam a stream of running water,")
    assert section.comments[-1].text.endswith(
        "*When they had heard these words, they marvelled, and left Him, and went their way,* "
        "carrying away their unbelief and wonder together.")
    whole = " ".join(c.text for c in section.comments)
    for printed in ("Cæsar", "Judæa", "pourtrayed", "tempters", "discerner", "self-satisfied", "populace"):
        assert printed in whole
    for slip in ("Caesar", "Csesar", "discemer", "  ", " ;", " ?"):
        assert slip not in whole



def test_the_sundays_after_the_test_sunday_are_checked_through_november_1():
    # October 25: Matthew 22:34-46; November 1 (the default set): Matthew 23:1-12.
    for reference, ids in (("Matthew 22:34-46", ["matthew-22-34-40", "matthew-22-41-46"]),
                           ("Matthew 23:1-12", ["matthew-23-1-4", "matthew-23-5-12"])):
        found = catena.sections_for(reference)
        assert [s.id for s in found] == ids
        assert all(s.checked for s in found)
    (aug,) = [c for c in catena.sections_for("Matthew 22:37")[0].comments if c.work == "Aug. de Doctr. Christ. i. 22."][:1]
    assert "final good¹;" in aug.text and aug.notes == ("¹ alia re frui.", "² al. bonum.")
````

- [ ] **Step 3: Write the module, the data files and the checklist**

**Create `backend/catena.py`:**

````python
"""The Catena Aurea on the four Gospels, as the app ships it (Voices of the Church V1).

The text of record is the 1841-45 Oxford printing (John Henry Parker), from the scans at
archive.org (owner's source decision, 2026-10-05). backend/data/catena/<gospel>.json holds every
section of the Catena (its verses, volume and printed pages); a section someone has checked
against the page images also holds its comments, exactly as printed. A section not yet checked
has no text in the file, so uncorrected OCR can never be shown (backend/scripts/catena_import.py
makes the drafts; the plan's "Checking a section" says how a section is checked).

Pure: no I/O but reading the data files (once each, lazily), no FastAPI.
"""
from __future__ import annotations

import json
import re
from dataclasses import dataclass
from functools import lru_cache
from pathlib import Path
from typing import Optional

from scripture_refs import parse_refs

FORMAT = 1
GOSPELS = ("Matthew", "Mark", "Luke", "John")
DATA_DIR = Path(__file__).resolve().parent / "data" / "catena"

PAGE_IMAGE_URL = "https://archive.org/download/{identifier}/page/n{leaf}.jpg"
PAGE_VIEW_URL = "https://archive.org/details/{identifier}/page/n{leaf}/mode/1up"


@dataclass(frozen=True)
class Volume:
    key: str
    gospel: str
    identifier: str          # the archive.org item
    title: str               # as the title page names the volume
    year: int                # the title page's year
    translator: Optional[str]  # as the volume names its translator; None when it names none
    holder: str              # whose copy was scanned


VOLUMES: tuple[Volume, ...] = (
    Volume("mt1", "Matthew", "catenaaureacomme00thomuoft", "Vol. I, St. Matthew, Part I", 1841,
           "Mark Pattison", "University of Toronto"),
    Volume("mt2", "Matthew", "a6788682p201thomuoft", "Vol. I, St. Matthew, Part II", 1841,
           "Mark Pattison", "Saint Mary's College of California"),
    Volume("mt3", "Matthew", "catenaurecommpt301thomuoft", "Vol. I, St. Matthew, Part III", 1842,
           "Mark Pattison", "University of Toronto"),
    Volume("mk", "Mark", "catenaaureacomme02thomuoft", "Vol. II, St. Mark", 1842,
           "John Dobree Dalgairns", "University of Toronto"),
    Volume("lk1", "Luke", "catenaaureacomme03thomuoft", "Vol. III, St. Luke, Part I", 1843,
           "Thomas Dudley Ryder", "University of Toronto"),
    Volume("lk2", "Luke", "p2catenaaureacom03thomuoft", "Vol. III, St. Luke, Part II", 1843,
           "Thomas Dudley Ryder", "University of Toronto"),
    Volume("jn1", "John", "catenaaureacomme04thomuoft", "Vol. IV, St. John, Part I", 1845,
           None, "University of Toronto"),
    Volume("jn2", "John", "p2catenaaureacom04thomuoft", "Vol. IV, St. John, Part II", 1845,
           None, "University of Toronto"),
)

# The father each printed label names, for display and (V2) the "after {Name}" credit.
FATHERS = frozenset({
    "Alcuin", "Ambrose", "Athanasius", "Augustine", "Basil", "Bede", "Cassian", "Chrysostom",
    "Cyprian", "Cyril of Alexandria", "Didymus", "Dionysius", "Epiphanius", "Eusebius",
    "Gregory of Nazianzus", "Gregory of Nyssa", "Gregory the Great", "Haymo", "Hilary", "Isidore",
    "Jerome", "John of Damascus", "Leo the Great", "Maximus", "Origen", "Peter Chrysologus",
    "Pseudo-Athanasius", "Pseudo-Augustine", "Pseudo-Chrysostom", "Pseudo-Jerome", "Pseudo-Origen",
    "Rabanus Maurus", "Remigius", "Severianus", "Theophylact", "Titus of Bostra",
    "a Greek expositor", "the Gloss",
})

_ITALICS = re.compile(r"\*[^*\n]+\*")


class CatenaDataError(ValueError):
    """A data file breaks the format: a bug in the file, caught by the tests before it ships."""


@dataclass(frozen=True)
class Comment:
    label: str               # as printed, in small capitals, with its stop ("Jerome;", "Chrys.")
    father: str              # one of FATHERS
    work: Optional[str]      # the margin reference beside the label ("Chrys. Hom. lxx."), as printed
    text: str                # as printed; *...* is italic; "\n\n" separates printed paragraphs
    notes: tuple[str, ...]   # the other margin notes beside it (Scripture references), as printed


@dataclass(frozen=True)
class Section:
    id: str
    gospel: str
    start: tuple[int, int]   # (chapter, verse)
    end: tuple[int, int]
    volume: Volume
    pages: tuple[int, int]   # the printed pages, first and last
    leaves: tuple[int, int]  # the scan's page indexes (archive.org's "n19"), first and last
    checked: Optional[dict]  # {"on": "YYYY-MM-DD", "by": "..."} once checked; None before
    comments: tuple[Comment, ...]

    @property
    def reference(self) -> str:
        (c1, v1), (c2, v2) = self.start, self.end
        if c1 == c2:
            return f"{self.gospel} {c1}:{v1}" + (f"-{v2}" if v2 != v1 else "")
        return f"{self.gospel} {c1}:{v1}-{c2}:{v2}"


def section_id(gospel: str, start: tuple[int, int], end: tuple[int, int]) -> str:
    """"matthew-22-15-22"; a section across chapters "matthew-16-28-17-9"."""
    (c1, v1), (c2, v2) = start, end
    tail = f"{v2}" if c1 == c2 else f"{c2}-{v2}"
    return f"{gospel.lower()}-{c1}-{v1}-{tail}"


def data_path(gospel: str) -> Path:
    return DATA_DIR / f"{gospel.lower()}.json"


def volume(key: str) -> Volume:
    for v in VOLUMES:
        if v.key == key:
            return v
    raise KeyError(key)


def page_image_url(v: Volume, leaf: int) -> str:
    return PAGE_IMAGE_URL.format(identifier=v.identifier, leaf=leaf)


def page_view_url(v: Volume, leaf: int) -> str:
    return PAGE_VIEW_URL.format(identifier=v.identifier, leaf=leaf)


def parse_gospel_reference(reference: str) -> list[tuple[tuple[int, int], tuple[int, int]]]:
    """The Gospel passages of a reference as (start, end) pairs, all of one Gospel (the first named)."""
    spans = [s for s in parse_refs(reference).spans if s.book in GOSPELS]
    if not spans:
        return []
    book = spans[0].book
    return [(s.start, s.end) for s in spans if s.book == book]


def gospel_of(reference: str) -> Optional[str]:
    spans = [s for s in parse_refs(reference).spans if s.book in GOSPELS]
    return spans[0].book if spans else None


def _comment(raw: dict, where: str) -> Comment:
    if set(raw) != {"label", "father", "work", "text", "notes"}:
        raise CatenaDataError(f"{where}: a comment has the keys label, father, work, text, notes")
    if raw["father"] not in FATHERS:
        raise CatenaDataError(f"{where}: unknown father {raw['father']!r}")
    text = raw["text"]
    if not isinstance(text, str) or not text.strip() or text != text.strip():
        raise CatenaDataError(f"{where}: empty text, or spaces around it")
    if re.search(r"(?<!\n)\n(?!\n)|\n{3,}|  ", text):
        raise CatenaDataError(f"{where}: a line break or a double space inside the text")
    if _ITALICS.sub("", text).count("*"):
        raise CatenaDataError(f"{where}: an italic marker without its pair")
    if not raw["label"] or (raw["work"] is not None and not raw["work"].strip()):
        raise CatenaDataError(f"{where}: an empty label or work")
    return Comment(raw["label"], raw["father"], raw["work"], text, tuple(raw["notes"]))


def parse(data: dict, gospel: str) -> tuple[Section, ...]:
    """A data file's sections, checked against the format (CatenaDataError on any break)."""
    if data.get("format") != FORMAT or data.get("gospel") != gospel:
        raise CatenaDataError(f"{gospel}: format {data.get('format')!r} for {data.get('gospel')!r}")
    out: list[Section] = []
    seen: set[str] = set()
    for raw in data["sections"]:
        start, end = tuple(raw["start"]), tuple(raw["end"])
        where = f"{gospel} {raw.get('id')}"
        base = section_id(gospel, start, end)
        if not re.fullmatch(re.escape(base) + r"(-[2-9])?", raw["id"]) or not start <= end:
            raise CatenaDataError(f"{where}: the id does not match the verses")
        if raw["id"] in seen:
            raise CatenaDataError(f"{where}: the id is used twice")
        seen.add(raw["id"])
        v = volume(raw["volume"])
        if v.gospel != gospel:
            raise CatenaDataError(f"{where}: volume {v.key} is not {gospel}")
        if out and (start, end) < (out[-1].start, out[-1].end):
            raise CatenaDataError(f"{where}: out of order after {out[-1].id}")
        status = raw["status"]
        if status == "checked":
            checked = raw["checked"]
            if not re.fullmatch(r"\d{4}-\d{2}-\d{2}", checked.get("on", "")) or not checked.get("by"):
                raise CatenaDataError(f"{where}: checked needs on (YYYY-MM-DD) and by")
            comments = tuple(_comment(c, where) for c in raw["comments"])
            if not comments:
                raise CatenaDataError(f"{where}: a checked section with no comments")
        elif status == "unchecked":
            if "comments" in raw or "checked" in raw:
                raise CatenaDataError(f"{where}: an unchecked section carries no text")
            checked, comments = None, ()
        else:
            raise CatenaDataError(f"{where}: status {status!r}")
        out.append(Section(raw["id"], gospel, start, end, v, tuple(raw["pages"]), tuple(raw["leaves"]),
                           checked, comments))
    return tuple(out)


@lru_cache(maxsize=len(GOSPELS))
def load(gospel: str) -> tuple[Section, ...]:
    """A Gospel's sections, read from its data file on first use and kept (the file ships with the app)."""
    return parse(json.loads(data_path(gospel).read_text(encoding="utf-8")), gospel)


def sections_for(reference: str) -> tuple[Section, ...]:
    """The sections that share at least one verse with the reference's Gospel passages, in order."""
    gospel = gospel_of(reference)
    if gospel is None:
        return ()
    spans = parse_gospel_reference(reference)
    return tuple(s for s in load(gospel)
                 if any(s.start <= end and start <= s.end for start, end in spans))


# The panel's credit line: the edition, the Gospel's volume, its translator as the volume names
# them, and the copies scanned (rights check §1; never CCEL's "William Whiston").
_GOSPEL_VOLUME = {"Matthew": "vol. I, St. Matthew", "Mark": "vol. II, St. Mark",
                  "Luke": "vol. III, St. Luke", "John": "vol. IV, St. John"}


def credit(gospel: str, shown: tuple[Section, ...] = ()) -> str:
    """"From the Catena Aurea of Thomas Aquinas, vol. I, St. Matthew, translated by Mark Pattison,
    edited by John Henry Newman (Oxford: John Henry Parker, 1841-42). Scanned from the University
    of Toronto's copy at archive.org." The copies are those of the sections shown (else the
    Gospel's)."""
    volumes = [v for v in VOLUMES if v.gospel == gospel]
    years = sorted({v.year for v in volumes})
    span = str(years[0]) if len(years) == 1 else f"{years[0]}-{str(years[-1])[2:]}"
    translator = volumes[0].translator
    translated = f"translated by {translator}" if translator else "translator not named in the volume"
    holders = list(dict.fromkeys(s.volume.holder for s in shown)) or list(dict.fromkeys(v.holder for v in volumes))
    copies = " and ".join(f"{h}'s" for h in holders) + (" copy" if len(holders) == 1 else " copies")
    return (f"From the Catena Aurea of Thomas Aquinas, {_GOSPEL_VOLUME[gospel]}, {translated}, edited by "
            f"John Henry Newman (Oxford: John Henry Parker, {span}). Scanned from the {copies} at archive.org.")
````

**Create `backend/data/catena/README.md`:**

````markdown
# The Catena Aurea's text (Voices of the Church)

One file per Gospel: `matthew.json`, `mark.json`, `luke.json`, `john.json`. The text of record is
the 1841-45 Oxford printing (John Henry Parker), as scanned at archive.org (owner's source
decision, 2026-10-05; `docs/superpowers/specs/2026-10-05-voices-rights-check.md`). The format is
`backend/catena.py`'s (checked when a file loads and by `backend/tests/test_catena_data.py`), and
the design is `docs/superpowers/specs/2026-10-05-voices-of-the-church-design.md`.

Every section of the Catena is listed with its verses, volume, printed pages and scan pages. Only a
section someone has **checked against the page images** has text (`"status": "checked"`); the app
shows "Not yet transcribed for this passage." for the others. Never put unchecked text here.

## Checking a section

1. Get the OCR of the eight volumes and the drafts (once; the cache is any directory outside the
   repo, about 100 MB):
   `.venv/bin/python backend/scripts/catena_import.py fetch --cache ~/catena-cache`, then
   `.venv/bin/python backend/scripts/catena_import.py draft --cache ~/catena-cache Matthew`
   (about a minute and a half a Gospel).
2. `.venv/bin/python backend/scripts/catena_import.py show --cache ~/catena-cache "Matthew 22:34-40"`
   prints the section's cleaned draft and the addresses of its page images
   (`https://archive.org/download/<item>/page/n<leaf>.jpg`). Save the draft section as a file of
   its own (one JSON object, as the data file holds a section).
3. Open each page image at a size where the italics and the small letters are clear (a band of
   about a third of the page, 1500 px wide). For each comment, against the page:
   - the label as printed ("Pseudo-Chrys.", "Jerome;"), the father it names (`father`, one of
     `catena.FATHERS`; "Id." is the father before it);
   - the margin reference beside the label (`work`, as printed: "Chrys. Hom. lxx."), and any other
     margin note beside the comment (`notes`: "1 Tim. 4, 3.");
   - the text, word by word: spelling, capitals and punctuation as printed; "æ" where printed;
     a word the line end broke joined (the hyphen kept only where the word is hyphenated anyway,
     "first-fruits"); no space before ";", ":", "?", "!"; straight quotes;
   - the italics, `*...*`, starting and ending where the printing's do;
   - a printed paragraph break inside one father's comment as a blank line (`\n\n`);
   - the OCR's usual slips: "rn" read as "m" ("discemer"), "ii" as "u", "æ" as "se" or "&", a
     dropped or added comma, a margin note's letters mixed into the text, two words joined or a
     word left in two halves.
4. Check the section's first and last verse and its printed pages against the page.
5. Set `"status": "checked"` and `"checked": {"on": "<YYYY-MM-DD>", "by": "<who>, word by word
   against the page images"}` (no email address), then
   `.venv/bin/python backend/scripts/catena_import.py index --cache ~/catena-cache Matthew --checked <file>`.
6. Add the section's id to `CHECKED` in `backend/tests/test_catena_data.py`, run
   `.venv/bin/python -m pytest -q backend/tests/test_catena.py backend/tests/test_catena_data.py backend/tests/test_catena_import.py`,
   and commit with the page image addresses in the message.

An OCR slip that recurs goes into `SLIPS` (or `MARGIN_SLIPS`) in `backend/scripts/catena_import.py`,
so later drafts have it right.
````
